Remark 7.13

(Rank drops of
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})
yield lower bounds on the filtration)
.

Observe that the total rank of the vector spaces
QH^{*}(Y)\cong\oplus_{\alpha}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})[-\mu_{\alpha}]
and
HF^{*}(\lambda H)\cong\oplus_{\alpha}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})[-\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})]
are the same, but the map
c_{\lambda}^{*}
in (
63
) is grading-preserving. Thus the more
\mu_{\alpha}-\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})
drops in
\lambda
(without a compensating jump from a different value of
\alpha
), the better the lower bound on the rank of
\ker c_{\lambda}^{*}
(this also works without the odd-cohomology vanishing assumption of

.(

)). This yields
1.32
(the maximum is due to
\mathscr{F}^{\varphi}_{\lambda}\subset\mathscr{F}^{\varphi}_{\gamma}
for
\lambda<\gamma
).

Considering the last remark (or equally, using
1.32

we get:

Corollary 7.14
.

Given a weight-
s
CSR
Y,

\mathscr{F}_{{1}/{s}}^{\varphi}\supset H^{\geq 2}(Y),\ \mathscr{F}_{{2}/{s}}^{\varphi}=H^{*}(Y).

In particular,
\mathscr{F}_{1}^{\varphi}=H^{*}(Y)
for

and

\mathscr{F}_{1}^{\varphi}\supset H^{\geq 2}(Y),\ \mathscr{F}_{2}^{\varphi}=H^{*}(Y)
for

7.5.
Computation of the continuation maps

Following
[
Rit10
, Sec.3.2-3.3]
, in situations where the Floer action functional
A_{H}
is well-defined, for continuations solutions
v
there is a difference between the (geometric) energy
E(v):=\int|\partial_{s}v|^{2}\,ds\wedge dt=A_{H_{-}}(x_{-})-A_{H_{+}}(x_{+})+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}}\partial_{s}H_{s}(v)\,ds\wedge dt
and the topological energy
E_{0}(v)=A_{H_{-}}(x_{-})-A_{H_{+}}(x_{+})
, where
x_{\pm}(t)=v(\pm\infty,t)
. Continuation maps count solutions with Novikov weight
T^{E_{0}(v)}
. One can write this without reference to
A_{H}
,

\textstyle E_{0}(v)=E(v)-\int_{\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}}\partial_{s}H_{s}(v)\,ds\wedge dt=\int v^{*}(\omega-dK\wedge dt),

abbreviating
K:\mathbb{R}\times Y\to\mathbb{R}
,
K(s,y):=H_{s}(y)
.
If
\partial_{s}H_{s}\leq 0
, then
E_{0}(v)\geq E(v)\geq 0
.
If a
1
-orbit
x
is in a region where
\partial_{s}H_{s}=0
, then
x
is a
1
-orbit of
H_{\pm}=H_{\pm\infty}
, and

E_{0}(v_{x})=0
for the “constant” continuation solution
v_{x}(s,t)=x(t)
.

Consider homotopies of type
H_{s}=\lambda_{s}H
with
\lambda_{s}
non-increasing. Add a constant to
H
if necessary, so that
H\geq 0
, then
\partial_{s}H_{s}\leq 0
. There can be “constant” continuation solutions with
E_{0}(v_{x})>0
: for homotopies
H_{s}=\lambda_{s}H
with
\lambda_{s}
non-increasing,
H\geq 0
, and
x\in\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}\subset\mathrm{Crit}(H)
, let
\lambda_{\pm}:=\lambda_{\pm\infty}
, then

E_{0}(v_{x})=H_{\alpha}\cdot(\lambda_{-}-\lambda_{+}),

where
H_{\alpha}:=H(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})
.
Despite
\partial_{s}H_{s}\leq 0
, one cannot exclude the possibility of non-constant continuation solutions
v
of the same (or possibly lower)
E_{0}(v)
value than
E_{0}(v_{x})
. Indeed, all continuation solutions in a small tubular neighbourhood of
x\in\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
will have the same
E_{0}
value.
110
110

110

\omega=d\theta
is exact in such a neighbourhood, so
A_{H}(x):=-\int x^{*}\theta+\int H(x)\,dt
and
A_{H_{-}}(x_{-})-A_{H_{+}}(x_{+})=H_{\alpha}\cdot(\lambda_{-}-\lambda_{+})
.

It seems that the contribution of a regular constant continuation solution might be unexpectedly cancelled in the continuation map count. We can exclude this for
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
: as
111
111

111

Alternatively, replacing
\lambda_{s}H
by
H_{s}:=\lambda_{s}(H-H_{\min})
does not affect
X_{H_{s}}
(the Floer continuation solutions are the same, but we count them with shifted weights). The minimisers of
E_{0}(v)
are then constant continuation solutions
v_{x}
at
x\in\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
, which have
E_{0}(v_{x})=0
. Finally, adding a constant
C
to
H
corresponds to a continuation map
T^{C}\cdot\mathrm{id}
. We cannot do this for general
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}\neq\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
as
\partial_{s}(\lambda_{s}(H-H_{\alpha}))
is not
\leq 0
everywhere.

H\geq H_{\min},

(64)

-\int\partial_{s}H_{s}(v)\,ds\wedge dt\geq-H_{\min}\cdot\int\partial_{s}\lambda_{s}\,ds=(\lambda_{-}-\lambda_{+})H_{\min}=:\delta_{\min}.

So constant continuation solutions are counted with weight
T^{\delta_{\min}}
; any other with
T^{>\delta_{\min}}.
So it is convenient to choose
H_{\min}=0
, thus
\delta_{\min}=0
, by adding a constant to
H
.
As in
5.7
, let

\lambda_{\alpha}:=\min\{\tfrac{1}{|k|}:h_{k}^{\alpha}\neq 0\textrm{ for }k\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\}=1/(\textrm{maximal absolute weight of }\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}).

Similarily to
7.11
, when
H^{*}(Y)
lies in even degrees, Floer continuation maps become:

(65)

\displaystyle\psi_{\gamma,\lambda}

\displaystyle:HF^{*}(\lambda H)\cong\oplus_{\beta}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})[-\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})]\to\oplus_{\beta}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})[-\mu_{\gamma}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})]\cong H^{*}(\gamma H).

Proposition 7.15
.

Let
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}:=\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
here. Suppose
H^{*}(Y)
lies in even degrees.
If for each weight
k
of
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
in (
39
) there are no integers in the interval
(|k|\lambda,|k|\gamma)
, then
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})=\mu_{\gamma}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})
and the matrix for
the part of
(
65
) given by
H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})[-\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})]\to\oplus_{\beta}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})[-\mu_{\gamma}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})]
,
has the form

(66)

T^{\delta_{\alpha}}\cdot\left(\mathrm{id}_{\alpha}+T^{>0}\textrm{--terms}\right),\,\textrm{where }\delta_{\alpha}:=(\gamma-\lambda)\cdot H_{\alpha},

and where
\mathrm{id}_{\alpha}
denotes the identity map on
H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})
-classes. In particular, in the notation of
Section

7.3
,

\mathrm{ini}(\ker\psi_{\gamma,\lambda})\subset\oplus_{\beta\neq\alpha}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta};\mathbb{B})[-\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})].

Proof.

We will use the Morse–Bott model for Floer cohomology, which counts “cascades”: this is explained in detail in the Appendix in
[
RŽ24b
]
. In particular, a basis of cycles for
H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta};\mathbb{B})
arises from Morse cohomology over
\mathbb{B}
for auxiliary Morse functions
f_{\beta}:\mathfrak{F}_{\beta}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Continuation maps in the Morse–Bott model count cascades in which a Floer continuation cylinder must be present.
The homotopy of Hamiltonians used is
\lambda_{s}H
where
\lambda_{s}=\gamma
for
s\ll 0
;
\lambda_{s}=\lambda
for
s\gg 0
; and
\partial_{s}\lambda_{s}\leq 0
.
Thus
\partial_{s}H_{s}\leq 0
, so
continuation cascades will be counted with a factor given by a non-negative power of
T
.
By (
64
), only the constant cascades with asymptotes in
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
are counted with power
T^{\delta_{\alpha}}
, any other cascade is counted with a power
T^{>\delta_{\alpha}}
.

Thus, to prove the claim, it suffices to explain why a constant cascade is regular. We consider the constant continuation cylinder at each constant
1
-orbit at
x\in\mathrm{Crit}(f_{\alpha})\subset\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
, where
f_{\alpha}:\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}\to\mathbb{R}
is the auxiliary Morse function used in the Morse–Bott–Floer model.
The local model near
x
is described by the weight decomposition:

\mathbb{C}^{d}\oplus\bigoplus_{i}\mathbb{C}_{w_{i}}

where
d=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
, and
\mathbb{C}_{w_{i}}
denotes a copy of
\mathbb{C}
with the weight
w_{i}\neq 0
action. Thus

\textstyle H_{s}=\pi\lambda_{s}\sum w_{i}|z_{i}|^{2}.

The Floer equation therefore decouples, and we reduce to considering the continuation map in
\mathbb{C}
,

HF^{*}(\mathbb{C};\pi w_{i}\lambda|z_{i}|^{2})\to HF^{*}(\mathbb{C},\pi w_{i}\gamma|z_{i}|^{2})

for
\pi w_{i}\lambda_{s}|z_{i}|^{2}
. One approach is to verify the regularity of the constant Floer continuation solution at
0
directly. Indirectly, we just need to show that map, viewed as local low-energy Floer cohomologies, is an isomorphism.
But this is known:
112
112

112

The continuation map only depends on the two slopes at infinity. Suppose
w_{i}>0
. We use instead a Hamiltonian
H_{w_{i}\gamma}
on
\mathbb{C}
obtained from
H_{w_{i}\lambda}:=\pi w_{i}\lambda|z_{i}|^{2}
by increasing the slope
away from a neighbourhood of
0
. A monotone homotopy
H_{s}
from
H_{w_{i}\lambda}
to
H_{w_{i}\gamma}
satisfies the maximum principle, so continuation solutions lie in the region where
H_{s}=H_{w_{i}\gamma}=H_{w_{i}\gamma}
is
s
-independent, as
H_{w_{i}\gamma}
does not have any new
1
-orbits compared to
H_{w_{i}\lambda}
. Those solutions cannot be rigid due to
s
-reparametrisation unless they are constant at
0
. Finally the constant
0
is regular for
H_{w_{i}\lambda}
(which equals
H_{s}
near
0
). For
w_{i}<0
,
H_{w_{i}\gamma}
is obtained by decreasing the negative slope of
H_{w_{i}\lambda}
at infinity, so the homotopy
H_{s}
is not monotone, but we are only interested in the local low-energy Floer continuation solutions (which is meaningful by using the monotonicity lemma
[
Rit14
, Lem.33]
), and the same argument shows that the constant solution at
0
is regular.
Indeed, the local low-energy continuation map is inverse to the continuation map
HF^{*}(H_{w_{i}\gamma})\to HF^{*}(H_{w_{i}\lambda})
in the reverse direction which involves a monotone homotopy and (by the previous argument) just counts the solution at
0
.

the map is an isomorphism precisely if the
1
-periodic
S^{1}
-action on
\mathbb{C}
does not have periodic orbits with period inside
(w_{i}\lambda,w_{i}\gamma)
, equivalently
(w_{i}\lambda,w_{i}\gamma)\cap\mathbb{Z}=\emptyset
. The latter holds by the assumption.

The final claim now follows:
if the initial part (in the sense of
Section

7.3
) of a class in
\oplus_{\beta}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})[-\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})]
is in
H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})[-\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})]
, then its image under
c_{\lambda}^{*}
cannot be cancelled by other contributions, as those arise with higher order
T
-terms.
∎

Corollary 7.16
.

If
H^{*}(Y)
lies in even degrees, and
\lambda<\lambda_{\min}
, then
c_{\lambda}^{*}:H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\min})\to HF^{*}(H_{\lambda})
is injective, so
\mathscr{F}_{\lambda}^{\varphi}\cap H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\min})=\{0\}
.
Therefore, in the notation of
Section

7.3
,

{\mathscr{F}_{\mathbb{B},\lambda}}^{\varphi}\subset\oplus_{\beta\neq\min}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta};\mathbb{B})[-\mu_{\beta}]\qquad\textrm{and }\qquad{\mathscr{F}_{\mathbb{B},\lambda}}^{\varphi}\otimes_{\mathbb{B}}\mathbb{K}\cong\mathscr{F}_{\lambda}^{\varphi}\subset\mathbb{K}\cdot({\mathscr{F}_{\mathbb{B},\lambda}}^{\varphi}+\oplus_{\beta}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta};\mathbb{K}_{>0})).

Without assumptions on
H^{*}(Y)
, if
c_{1}(Y)=0
,
\lambda<\lambda_{\min}
and
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})\geq 0
for all
\beta
, then
1\notin\mathscr{F}^{\varphi}_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

The first part is immediate from
7.15
:
if the initial part (in the sense of
Section

7.3
) of a class in
\oplus_{\beta}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})[-\mu_{\beta}]
involves a non-zero entry in
H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\min};\mathbb{B})
, then the image under
c_{\lambda}^{*}
of that entry cannot be cancelled by other contributions, as those arise with higher order
T
-terms.

For the second part, let
1
be the unit cycle in
QH^{*}(Y)
. Its image
c^{*}_{\lambda}(1)=T^{\delta_{\min}}\cdot(1+T^{>0}\textrm{-terms})\in CF^{*}(\lambda H)
(by
7.15
) is a cycle but we need to ensure it is not a boundary. The conditions
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})\geq 0
ensure no chains in
CF^{*}(\lambda H)
have negative grading, in particular grading
-1
(which could give rise to a non-trivial differential killing the unit due to high-energy Floer trajectories).
∎

Remark 7.17
.

We expected
7.15
to hold for all
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}

with one end on

(currently we obtain this only for

via

).

Definition 7.18
.

\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
is
p
-stable
if
\tfrac{1}{p}\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}\cap(
weights(
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}))=\emptyset
. (So:
p\neq\tfrac{m}{k}
, for

weights(
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
),
m\in\mathbb{Z}
).

Proposition 7.19
.

Let
H^{*}(Y)
lie only in even degrees.
Suppose that
for each weight
m
of
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
there are no integers in the interval
(|m|\lambda,|m|\gamma)
,
so
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})=\mu_{\gamma}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})
.
If
\gamma-\lambda
is sufficiently small, then the part of the map
\psi_{\gamma,\lambda}
in (
65
) given by
H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})[-\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})]\to\oplus_{\beta}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})[-\mu_{\gamma}(\mathfrak{F}_{\beta})]
equals (
66
).

For
\lambda=p^{-}
,
\gamma=p^{+}
, and
p
-stable
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
the two indices
\mu_{p^{\pm}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})
are equal, and
\psi_{p^{+},p^{-}}
restricted to
\oplus_{p\textrm{-stable }\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}}H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})[-\mu_{p^{-}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})]
is injective.
For the smallest period
p>0
for which
S^{1}
-orbits exist, the total rank
\|\mathscr{F}^{\varphi}_{p}\|\leq\|H^{*}(Y)\|-\sum_{(p\textrm{-stable }\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})}\|H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})\|.

Proof.

As
\partial H_{s}\leq 0
, a monotonicity lemma argument (e.g.
[
Rit14
, Lem.33]
) implies that given a tubular neighbourhood
N_{\alpha}
of
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
, there is an
\epsilon>0
, so that continuation solutions with at least one end on
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
, and not entirely contained in

, have energy

If
\gamma-\lambda
is small enough so that
\delta_{\alpha}
in (
66
) satisfies
\delta_{\alpha}<\epsilon
, then
E_{0}(v)>\delta_{\alpha}
for all cascades with ends on
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
except possibly for those in
N_{\alpha}
.
Cascades in
N_{\alpha}
, for small
N_{\alpha}
and small energy, involve the low-energy local Floer cohomology of
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
(see the Appendix in
[
RŽ24b
]
). But the assumption implies that those local groups are isomorphic under the continuation map (cf. the previous
Footnote

112
). So the total contribution of the continuation cascades in
N_{\alpha}
(all contributing with weight
T^{\delta_{a}}
) is the identity continuation map times
T^{\delta_{a}}
.

The second claim follows:
\psi_{p^{+},p^{-}}(T^{-\delta_{\alpha}}x_{\alpha})=x_{\alpha}+(T^{>0}
-terms) for
x_{\alpha}\in H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})[-\mu_{p^{-}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})]
where
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
is
p
-stable. For the restricted map in the claim,

\psi_{p^{+},p^{-}}(\sum n_{\alpha}T^{-\delta_{\alpha}}x_{\alpha})=\sum n_{\alpha}(x_{\alpha}+T^{>0}
-terms), which is non-zero unless all
n_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{K}
are zero.
113
113

113

if the minimal order
T
term arising in the
\psi
-output is
T^{\min}\sum b_{\alpha}x_{\alpha}
, where
b_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{B}
are in the base field, then this must arise for the subset of the
n_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{K}
of the form
T^{\min}(b_{\alpha}+T^{>0}
-terms). Then use that the
x_{\alpha}
are independent over
\mathbb{B}
.

The final claim follows by rank-nullity: by stability in (
15
),
c_{p^{-}}^{*}
is an isomorphism, and by the second claim:
\|\mathrm{rk}\,\psi_{p^{+},p^{-}}\|\geq\sum_{(p\textrm{-stable }\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})}\|H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})\|
.
∎

Remark 7.20
.

When
\lambda
passes a “critical time”
\tfrac{k}{m}
, where
m
is a weight of
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
, the index
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})
often drops. Recall, when
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
does not have negative weights
-mb
for
b\in\mathbb{N}
, it is the minimal locus of the restricted moment map
H|_{Y_{m,\beta}}
for the torsion
m
-submanifold containing
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
, and we call
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}

m
-minimal. In that case,
Y_{m,\beta}
near
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
is a complex vector bundle over
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
, and we denote
\text{rk}(Y_{m,\beta})
its complex rank. We show that
\mu_{\lambda}(\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha})
drops precisely by
2\mathrm{rk}(Y_{m,\beta})
when
\lambda
passes the critical time. Working within that bundle, there is an
m
-th root
\varphi^{1/m}
of the action on
Y_{m,\beta}
; that corresponds to a full rotation in
Y_{m,\beta}
so it has an associated class
Q_{\varphi^{1/m}}\in QH^{*}(Y_{m,\beta})
. One expects by
[
Rit14
]
that
Q_{\varphi^{1/m}}
is the Euler class
e(Y_{m,\beta})
, at least up to higher order
T
terms. This led us to formulate
1.46
.

Lemma 7.21
.

\lambda_{min}=1\Leftrightarrow\mu=\mathrm{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{F}_{\min}\Leftrightarrow(\textrm{all nonzero weights of }\mathfrak{F}_{\min}\textrm{ are }+1)
.

Proof.

There are
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}Y-\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
non-zero weights for
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
, they are positive integers (as
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}=\min H
) and their sum is
\mu
(
5.1
).
So
\mu=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}Y-\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{F}_{\min}\Leftrightarrow
positive weights
=+1
.
∎

Corollary 7.22
.

Let
Y
be a weight-2 CSR with
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}=\{\mathrm{point}\},

or a weight-1 CSR.

Then
\mathscr{F}^{\varphi}_{\lambda}\cap H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\min})=\{0\}
for
\lambda<1,
in particular
1\in H^{0}(Y)
survives until time-1.

Proof.

Given a weight-2 CSR with
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}=\{\mathrm{point}\},
due to (
71
) it has only
+1 weights, thus
7.21
applies and
the claim follows from
7.16
(
\lambda<1
excludes
\alpha=\min
as
\lambda_{\min}=1
).
For a weight-1 CSR,
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
is an
\omega_{J}
-Lagrangian (the “minimal Lagrangian” of
[
Ž22
]
), thus
half of its weights are equal to 0, and the other half equal to +1 due to (
71
),
thus the same argument applies.
∎

Theorem 7.23

(

it is not difficult to verify that the construction and properties of this continue to hold in our setup, by using the maximum principle from

Lemma 7.25
.

Let
Y
be a symplectic
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-manifold, and

\mathfrak{F}_{\min}

its minimal component (
3.22
).

(67)

\mathrm{PD}[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]\neq 0\in H^{*}(Y)\iff e(U_{\min})\neq 0\in H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\min}),

where
e(U_{\min})
is the Euler class of the normal bundle of
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
(see
4.8
).

In particular,

\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{F}_{\min}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\dim_{\mathbb{C}}Y
is a necessary condition for the non-vanishing (
67
).

Proof.

The last claim follows for degree reasons:
e(U_{\min})\in H^{2\,\mathrm{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}\,\mathfrak{F}_{\min}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\min})
.
The identification of
U_{\min}:=W^{s}_{-\nabla H}(\mathfrak{F}_{\min})
with the normal bundle of
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
follows from

4.11

(letting
m=1
).

By Poincaré–Lefschetz duality,

\mathrm{PD}[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]\neq 0\in H^{*}(Y)
if and only if the locally finite (lf) cycle
[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]\in H_{*}^{lf}(Y)
has non-trivial intersection number with some cycle
C\in H_{*}(Y)
.
We can perturb the lf-cycle
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
in
U_{\min}
as the image of a generic smooth section of the normal bundle to
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
.
We can perturb the metric in the complement of a small neighbourhood of
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
to make the flow of
H
Morse-Smale, and we can perturb
H
to make it Morse away from
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
. Then by Morse theory the cycles in
H_{*}(Y)
that do not come from the inclusion
H_{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\min})\to H_{*}(Y)
can be represented as pseudo-cycles by linear combinations of the unstable manifolds of the critical points of
H
not in
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
(the algebro-geometrical analogue of this is described in
[
Ž22
, Sec.2.2]
). These unstable manifolds correspond to submanifolds
116
116

116

using the Morse–Smale property, and for the codimension claim we use that the unstable manifold of a Morse critical point
p
is the Morse index, and that Morse indices will be
\leq 2\dim_{\mathbb{C}}Y-2
by
3.22
and non-compactness of
Y
.

in

N^{+}
of real codimension at least
2
, where
N^{+}\subset TY|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\min}}
is the subbundle where
\mathrm{Hess}(H)
is positive definite.
It follows that we can construct a smooth section
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}\to U_{\min}
of the normal bundle that avoids the closures of those pseudo-cycles.
Therefore, we have built an lf-homologous perturbation of
[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]
which can only intersect the cycles in
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
. This implies that
[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]\neq 0\in H_{*}^{lf}(Y)
if and only if
[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]\neq 0\in H_{*}^{lf}(U_{\min})
.
Finally, by the proof of
[
Rit14
, Thm.67]
, PD of
[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]\in H_{2\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{F}_{\min}}^{lf}(U_{\min})
equals the
pull-back in
H^{2\,\mathrm{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}\,\mathfrak{F}_{\min}}(U_{\min})
of
e(U_{\min})
.
∎

Proposition 7.26
.

Suppose

is Kähler with
c_{1}(Y)=0
or
non-compact Fano
117
117

117

see

, Lem.1.6]

for how to correctly interpret the Maslov index when

.

and

\mu=\mathrm{codim}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathfrak{F}_{\min}\;\;\textrm{ and }\;\;\mathrm{PD}[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]\neq 0\in H^{2\mu}(Y).

Then

Q_{\varphi}=\mathrm{PD}[\mathfrak{F}_{\min}]+(
terms with
T^{>0})\neq 0\in QH^{2\mu}(Y).

Proof.

This will rely on
[

, Lem.1.6]
118
118

118

In that lemma,

needs to be connected, otherwise the statement of

is the absolute minimum of the Hamiltonian

of the action

, as will become clear from our discussion of

-weights in the current proof.
(that was stated in the non-compact Fano case, but the written proof for

works also in the non-compact CY case). The contribution

arises from certain constant sections counted by the GW-interpretation of

. The additional contributions to

come from: (1) other moduli spaces of constant sections that sweep out lf-cycles inside the other fixed components

(after cutting down the moduli space using obstruction bundle techniques

To justify that (1) and (2) contribute with strictly positive powers of

mentioned in

[

, Sec.3.1]
. One actually works with a cover of the free loop space of

, obtained by capping off loops by discs, and identifying two discs if they give rise to a sphere class in

, which are
\pi_{2}(Y)
classes on which
\omega
and

vanish. A lift
\widetilde{\varphi}
is determined by knowing how it acts on one such capping. We choose
\widetilde{\varphi}
as in
[
Rit14
, Sec.7.8]
by declaring that it sends the constant cap at a fixed point in
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
to itself.
When constructing
Q_{\varphi}
we “normalise” the Hamiltonian of the action: we use
K:=H-H(\mathfrak{F}_{\min})
so that the Hamiltonian
K=0
on
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
. The same argument as in
[
Rit14
, first Lemma in Sec.7.8]
119
119

119

In the proof of that Lemma,
\phi
is a function that increases from
0
to
1
(there is a sign change compared to the
\phi
in the proof of

, Lm.40]

over

).
then applies in our setting: the symplectic form

on the Hamiltonian fibration used to construct

evaluates to zero on the class of the constant section

at a point of
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
. On the other hand, running the computation of

, first Lemma in Sec.7.8]
for the class of a constant section

at a point

, we get
120
120

120

This is consistent with the fact that the induced “cap” for the constant loop at a point of
\mathfrak{F}_{\alpha}
(having chosen the constant “cap” at points of
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
) is described by a succession of pseudoholomorphic spheres as in

starting from
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
and ending at

\widetilde{\Omega}(s_{\alpha})=K(x_{\alpha})>0=K(\mathfrak{F}_{\min})

the

We use a simplified Novikov field compared to

for

(see

) with weight

(68)

T^{\omega(\gamma)}=T^{\int\!s_{\min}^{*}\widetilde{\Omega}}\cdot T^{\omega(\gamma)}=T^{\int\!s^{*}\widetilde{\Omega}}.

It follows that all sections, except for constants at
\mathfrak{F}_{\min}
, are counted with a
